In fact, the joy which is transcendental is the gift of the Lord Himself as the supreme spiritual reality and it can never come from any earthly object by any process of transmutation.

The rhetoricians however, do not accept bhakti as rasa on the ground that the common basis of a relishable rasa is the affectionate human relationship standing on the same level.

It, in fact, does not hold good in respect of Bhakti or devotion to a deity who is above the common level of human beings. Jiva Gosvami also maintains that it is more true in the case of other gods who belong to mundane sphere and not in case of supreme deity, i.e., Lord Krsna. He also maintains that Bhagavat-priti can be rightly regarded as a sthayibhava because as Priti. it has bhavatva and it has also all the characteristics of a sthayi-bhava mentioned in the Sanskrit poetics.

As regards Lord Krsna, He is the supreme person, i.e., Bhagavan Himself. He is the embodiment of all rasasand He is the real enjoyer of relish of his nature as has been described in the Bhagavata about all his pastimes. It is, therefore, said that Bhakti being the function of His intrinsic power does not belong to empirical plane. It cannot be born of or generated by anything other than the Lord Himself. Strictly speaking, devotion to Lord is the only rasa according to the Gaudiyas. In reality, such devotion is the inherent delight of the Lord in His own perfection ever flowing as it were, into finite creatures. In this sense, we may say that man’s love for God is the reflection of the Love which inheres in the Lord Himself. This is a mediated delight which the Lord enjoys even more than the immediate delight in His own Perfection. Bhakti is rasa because of transcendental quality that characterises it.

As regards the question of locus of Rasa in a literary composition, Jiva Gosvami refers to the four theories of rhetoricians. According to the first view, rasa exists in the original hero and heroine (anukarva) who are to be imitated by the actor and actress on the stage. The second group of alankarikas however, regard that the aethetic delight (rasa) lies in the actor and actress (anukartr) who imitate the original hero and heroine. There is however, a third view of rhetoricians who think that rasa or aesthetic delight has its locus in the heart of the audience or the spectators i.e., samajikas), but according to the fourth view, rasa exists not only in the audience or spectators but also in the actor and actress if they are man of literary taste (dvitiye sacetastvam). The last view has been accepted by the exponents of most of the secular poetics and thus they regard it as their ideal view.

The Gaudiyas, however, reject all the above-mentioned theories saying that it is not applicable to Bhakti-rasa which exists in all sentient bhaktas. The secular poetics failed to give sufficient reasons for their view about the locus of rasa. According to Jiva Gosvami, bhakti-rasa or aesthetic delight which is nothing but transcendental love of God, exists in the original hero and heroine (anukaryesu tatparikaresu) as in inherent quality of them. Being unlimited and transcendental in nature, it flows from the original hero who is no other than the Lord Himself, into the hearts of the actor and actress who are also none else than real bhaktas or aspirants following the way of bhakti for its own sake. It, further, flows away to the audience who are not only ordinary men of taste, but also the bhaktas themselves. It is because of the fact that none else has the capacity of realising bhakti-rasa properly if they are not real bhaktas.

The Gaudiyas, however, mention another important characteristic of bhakti-rasa viz. the locus (asraya) and the object of rasa must necessarily be sentient beings. There can be no experience of aesthetic delight arising out of insentient things. Physical objects may at best, be excitants (uddipaka) only for aesthetic delight, but they can never be objects (visya or alambana) of asethetic joy. That being so, all aesthetic experience of rasa is nothing but the love of God who is the Highest Person; thus, according to the Gaudiyas, the object of aesthetic delight is always Krsna, while the locus or asraya who has the experience of aesthetic delight is a bhakta or devotee.

There is, however, difference of opinion among the orthodox poetics about the enumeration of rasas. According to Bharata, there are only eight rasas namely, sringara, vira, raudra, bibhatsa, hasya, adabhuta, karuna and bhayanaka. Dandin has followed Bharata in this respect, while many other exponents of Sanskrit poetics however, differ from him. Acaryas like Udbhata, Rudrata and Jagannatha, etc. have added santa rasa in addition to the eight rasas accepted by Bharata. But Bhoja also accepts preyas and vats alaas rasas while Visvanatha adds only vatsalaas the ninth rasa.

But the Gaudiyas give a different enumeration of rasas. According to them, there is no rasa except the Bhakti-rasa. In other words, there is no possibility of aesthetic delight except through the devotion to Lord. The joys and sorrows of laukika-rati bear no aesthetic significance. They, in fact, relate to and arise out of the relation of the finite to the finite and there is no lokottara or transcendental element in such relationship. According to them, no aesthetic experience is possible without a lokottara or transcendental element in it. The Gaudiyas, however, accept twelve rasasin all. Among them, five rasas are called mukhya-bhakti-rasa viz. Santa.

Prita, Preyas, Vatsala and Madhura, while the other seven rasas are said to be of secondary type and are designated as gauna-bhakti rasa. In other words, according to the Gaudiyas seven out of eight rasas of the alankarikas (with the exception of Srngara rasa) are recognised as secondary rasas only.

We shall now describe these five primary bhakti rasas in some details. As the relation of locus (asraya) and the object (alambana) varies, so does the quality of bhakti or devotion as the resulting aesthetic experience.

**1. Santa-Rasa** When for example, Lord Krsna is contemplated as ananda-svarupa or the very essence of the true delight, there is an unruffled calm (sama) enjoyment of the Lord’s infinite joy and delight in the devotee, this devotion is called Santa-rasa which is also designated as jnana-bhakti mava-rasah by Jiva Gosvami.

The vibhavas of Santa rasa are (1) alambana four-armed Narayana and two kinds of Santa bhaktas. viz. atma-ramah. i.e., self-contented and self-satisfied saints like Sanaka, Sananadana, etc. and tapasah. etc.(2) Uddipana, i.e., listening to the Upanisadic teachings, residing in a lonely place, development of intellectual faculties, discussion of tattvas, keeping the company of jnani-bhaktas and participating in discussions on Brahma sutra, etc.

Its anubhavas are: Living like an avadhuta concentrating on the tip of the nose, walking with down caste eyes, displaying of the Jnana-mudra neither to hate the enemies of Krsna, nor to be attached with His devotees very much, attaching a great importance to the siddhata of the Jivan-muktas destruction of one’s ego, being irrespective of any kind of help from others, and observing silence. Its sattvika signsare: horripilation perspiration, trembling, but not fainting (pralayavina) etc.

Its Vyabhicariesare: self-disparagemen̮Prita-bhakti-rasa is the best of all rasas according to Sridhara and is regarded as the foundation of rati by the author of Namakaumudi. This is said to be of two kinds, viz., (i) sambharma Prita or respectful devotion, and gaurava Prita or devotional pride. The Vibhavas of the former are as follows:

(1) alambana - Krsna and His servants. He has two hands, but sometimes, He appears in four handed form before the followers of this rasa. The servants of Krsna (Dasah) may be of four kinds, (i), adhikrta (controlled), (ii) asruta (protected),(iii) parsada (retinue), and (iv)anuga (followers). (2) Uddipana: receiving Krsna’s favour in the shape of dust of His feet and remnants of His or His devotees food and association of His devotees, hearing of venu, glimpse of his smiling face, anubhavas are complete application of self to appointed duties, without having any jealousy thereof, friendly relations with the devotees of the Lord, etc.

Its sattvakas are, almost all those horriplations etc. mentioned above, vyabhicari-bhavas are thirty three in number which include harsa, gaurva, dhrti, nirveda, etc.. Its sthayi bhava is sambhrama, prabhuta-jnana having knowledge of His mangificance, and respectful attitude.

The second variety of Prita-Rasa includes it sthayi-bhava as pride due to a feeling of being favoured dependent of Krsna.

Its vibhavas are: almbana: Krsna’s various qualities such as His being a great protector, a great preceptor, having a great wisdom and glory, and the lalyas His loving youngers and sons etc.

Its uddipanas are: having affectionate loving attitude of the Lord and His smile etc.

Its anubhavas are: sitting on a low seat before Krsna, following the steps of the preceptor as it were, to carry his effects and to give up arbitrary disposition or nature.

Its sattvakas are as mentioned above and vyabhicaris are those mentioned in sambharama Priti and special gaurava Priti may generate preman, (love), sneh (affection) and raga (attachment) under special circumstances.

Priti-bhakti-rasa leads to yoga (union), siddhi, (to obtain him after a long and eager expectation) and tusti (to regain Him after His disappearance).

**3. Preyo-bhakti-Rasa** or Sakhya bhakti-rasa or devotion as companionship of equals in position, relation or age. The distance which is servant has to observe towards his master is conspicuous by absence between one friend and the other. Though, there is no question of subservience between two play-fellows or friends, yet the service which a servant renders to his master, is not absent in the relation of fellowship of equals. If needed, the devotee of this type does not fail to render any service to his object of devotion. By doing service, he has no feeling of inferiority or sub-servience. Hence, an attitude of friendship is the sthayi-bhava of Prevo-bhakti-rasa or sakhya-bhakti-rasa .

Its vibhavas are: (1) Alambana - Krsna and His friends of the same age, having same dress (uniform) and same avudhas etc., either in vraja or in Dvaraka. The friend may be of four types (i) suhrta (slightly elder), sakhi (slightly younger), priya sakhi (same age), priya-narma-sakhi (helpers in affairs).

Its Uddipanas are (1) vayah or age, namely kaumara (1-5 years), pauganda (5-10 years), kaisorya (10-15 years), (ii) place, upto the age of ten in Vraja and thereafter either in Vraja or Mathura or Dvarka. (iii) Various kinds of dress suitable to age, (iv) instruments for playing venu, etc.Its anubhavas are various types of sports such as niyuddha, Kanduka, dyuta etc.Its sattvikas are the same as described above.

The Vyabhicari-bhavas are: all (mentioned above) excepting ugrata, trasa and alasya.

This kind of rasa may develop successively into pranay, preman, sneha, and raga.

**4. Vatsala bhakti-rasa** We have, again, another form of bhakti-rasa manifesting itself in the relation of parent and child. This is bhakti or devotion known as vatsalya rasa, or parental love. This includes, however, sakhya. dasya as well as santa rasa. The parental love of the devotee does not preclude the love of fellowship or love as service or again, the calm enjoyment of the object as the very essence of infinite delight.

Its sthayi-bhava is vatsalya or parental affection. Its vibhavas are: (1) alambana: Krsna and His elders, such as Nanda, Yasoda, Vasudeva, Devaki, etc.

(ii) Uddipana: Krsna’s young age (kumaradi vayah). His handsome appearance, childish pranks, smiles etc.

Its anubhavas are: smelling the head, (Siroghranam) feeling the body with the hands, blessings, giving directions, bringing up, and giving him good advice.

The satvikas are - all the eight satvikas described above plus flowing of milk due to motherly affection, hence the total number of these in this rasa is nine. The vvabicari are - all mentoned in the Priti, and apasmara (dementedness). It may develop into Preman, Sneha and Raga.

**5. Madhura-Bhakti-rasa** The fifth of the bhakti-rasa or primary forms of bhakti as rasa is Madhura-rasa or the love of one sex for the other. It may appear as the lawful conjugal love or as love of one’s beloved in utter disregard of all social conventions. The last is considered to be the devotion of the highest type. When one is prepared to face social opporbrium and risk, everything for the sake of the beloved-one, is a devotee of the highest type. Madhura-rasa does not preclude the other four kinds of primary rasas. One may even feel parental love for one’s beloved, and render upto him all manners of service when one’s beloved for example, is taken seriously ill.

The Madhura-bhakti rasa has been described briefly in the Bhakti-rasamrta-Sindhubut it has been dealt with by the author in detail, in his Ujjavala-nilmani. The word ujjavala implies madhura or transcendental amatory love of Radha and Krsna which is known as Bhakti-rasa-rat.

The Vibhavas of Madhura rati are (1) Alambana -Krsna as the object and His beloveds viz. Gopis as the locus of the emotion. He has many qualities and may appear as one of the four types of hero (i.e., Dhirodatta etc.) and may figure either as husband or as paramour or amant (upa-pati). He is the husband of the ladies of the Vraia-Gopis whose passion has overcome their sense of conventional duty and this establishes the superiority of Srangara-rasa among all rasas.

Among the beloveds of Krsna there are svakiyas and parakiyas. Svakiyas are those who are married to Krsna, viz. the sixteen thousand and one hundred and eight, at Mathura and Dvaraka. Though they are equal in beauty and qualities, still eight of them are foremost among them. Of the eight, again, Rukmani is the foremost in majesty, while Satvabhama is most favoured by fortune. Parakiya has been defined as when there is mutual surrender due to passion and the union is not sanctified by marriage, the lady is called parakiva. It is of two kinds, namely, Kanyaka (maiden) and parodha (married woman), but these married women, that is vraja-gopis never had any physical relation with their husbands.

Harivallabhas or the beloveds of Krsna are classifed into three categories of, viz., (1) sadhana-para. (2) devis and (3) nitya-priya.

(I) sadhana-para or sadhana-siddhas are those who have attained the present state of realization through practising the ways of devotion. Such Vallabhas may be either vauthiki i.e., of a group (under a yuthesvari) or ayauthiki. Among yauthiki, there are munis and the Upanisads, the former having become gopis according to the Padma Purana, while other Puranas state that the Upanisads had become Gopis.

ayauthikis are further classified as Pracina (old) and navya (young).

(II) Devis are those eternal beloveds of Lord Krsna who descended in the region of the gods (i.e., divi) for the pleasure of the Lord in the form of the ladies of Heaven.

(III) The third variety, viz. nitya priyah, however, involves the eternal beloveds of the Lord in Vraja, and among them, the chief are Radha and Candravali, having similar qualities as those of Lord Krsna . There are others viz. Visakha, Lalita, Syama, etc. There are hundreds of such nitya-sidhyas, who are all leaders of a vutha and are called (yuthadhipa). Of them, eight are foremost, and among them Radha and Candravali are at the top. But between Radha and Candravali, Radha is superior. Radha as the Vrndavanesvari and eternal consort of Krsna is the foremost beloved. She is identified with the Hladini Mahasakti of the Tantra. Her five kinds of companions, viz.sakhi, nitya-sakhi, prana-sakhi, priya-sakhi and parama-prestha-sakhi are also mentioned with their individual names.

Sri Visvanatha Cakravartin explains the five varieties of sakhis in his Ujjavala-nilmani-kirna as follows. There are a few those who are more partial to Lord Krsna are called sakhi viz. (Vrnda, Kundalata, etc.) and those who are more partial in their affection to Radha are called nitya-sakhis (viz. Kasturi, etc.). Among nitya-sakhis, those who are the chief are called prana-sakhis or Jivita-sakhvas (viz. Tulasi, Keli-Kandali, etc.). But those who have attained similar form are called priya-sakhis such as malati, Candralatika etc. The chief of these priya-sakhis are called parama-nrestha-sakhis such as Lalita, Visakha, etc. though they have equal feeling of affection for Radha and Krsna, but even then, they are more partial to Radha.

The beloved ladies of Vraja are further classified into eight groups based on the situation of their present state of relation with Krsna. These are viz. (i) abhisarika, (ii) vasaka-sajja, (iii) utkanthita, (iv) khandita. (v) viprala-badha, (vi) kalahantarita, (vii) prositabhartrka and (viii) svadhina-bhartrka.

All these may be again ranked according to their affection with which Krsna regards them, thus, they are viz., (a) uttama, (b) madhyama, (c) kanistha.

The beloved ladies of Krsna are also classifiedaccording to their attitude to their rivals. They are, viz. (i) svapaksa (interested in herself), suhrta-paksa (interested in her friend), does good to her friend or prevents evil befalling her, (iii) tatastha (indifferent) and (iv) vipaksa (hostile, harms her rivals and prevents good).

The Uddipana-vibhavas of Madhura-rati are circumscribed by the factors of time, place and circumstances, and depend on Krsna’s guna (qualities), cesta (explits), His prasadhana (toilette and personal adornment) and other miscellaneous factors. His qualities are sub-divided into kavika, vacika and manasah. Miscellanous factors include His smile, the fragrance of His body, His foot-prints, His beloved plants Tulasi, His instruments, flute, etc.

The Uddipana vibhava as also include the surroundings called tatastha (peripheral). These are dark clouds, lightning, the seasons of spring and autumn, full-moon, birds, etc.The anubhavas of madhura-rasa are (i) alarikara, (ii) udbhasvaras and (iii) vacikas.

The sattvikas are the usual eight, but here the psychological causes of such physiological changes are described as the cause of stambha are given as harsa, bhava, ascarva. etc.

All the usual vyabhicari-bhavas. such as nirveda, etc. have been included excepet ugrata and alasva. But extra element in this vyabhicari-bhavas are the four dasas (conditions) viz. (i) utpatti, (ii) sandhi, (iii) sabalata and (iv) santi.

Next comes the sthayi-bhava which in srangara-rasa is known as Madhura-rati. The basic formulation of this rati depends upon the following:

- (1) abhiyoga or manifestation of the feeling (bhava vyakti) personally or through an intermediary.
- (2) visaya or five sense objects, viz. sabda, sparsa, rupa, rasa, gandha.
- (3) sambandha: pride in beauty, family, etc.
- (4) abhimana : hankering after a particular object.
- (5) upama : resemblance however slight.
- (6) svabhava : subjective temperament depending upon the erotic processes of cognative impression (nisarga) and impressions created by confirmed habits (sanskara) and involuntary reflexes (svarup).

In the madhura-rati the beloved ladies are grouped into three types (i) sadharani, (ii) samanjasa and

(iii) samartha. They are compared to the increasing excellence of mani (jewel), cinta-mani (the wishing-jewel) and the kaustubha-mani (the jewel habitually worn by Krsna on His breast.

The first category consists of such beloved ladies who are concerned exclusively with their own gratification, but which can be through Krsna alone, e.g., Kubja. It extends up to the stage Preman.

In the second category, the beloved ladies are none else but Krsna’s queens only, who desired mutual gratification, their love was more deep (sandra) than that of a sadharani. It extends upto the anuraga stage.

The third category, viz. samartha nayikas are the gopis, who were concerned only with Krsna’s happiness, and had no sense of self-gratification because they had completely identified themselves with Krsna, their love was most deep-rooted (sandra-tama). This samartha-rati ultimately ripens into mahabhava.

The Madhura-rati has various stages of developments, according to the degree of its intensity. They are (1) preman, (2) sneha, (3) mana, (4) pranava,(5)raga, (6) anuraga, (7) bhava and (8) mahabhava. These have been compared with the increasing sweetness of (1) seed (bija), (2) sugarcane (iksuh), (3) sugarcane-juice (rasa),(4) molasses (guda), (5) khanda, (6) sugar (sarkara)

(7) sugarcandy (sita) and (8) refined candy (sitopala). Each succeeding one, while containing in the basic elements of the sweetness of the previous one is sweeter and purer than the previous one. The generic term for these inter-related emotions is also preman. However, they have to be considered individually, as this topic is probably the most important among Bhakti-rasa.

(1) Preman is the indissoluble bend of feeling that binds a couple through all adverse circumstances. It develops in three stages, namely manda (slight), madhya (medium) and praudha (mature).

(2) Sneha is the end-product of Preman when the heart melts in love. Here again, there are three types of nayikas, of which the kanistha, (junior) is satisfied with hearing of Him, the madhyama (the middle group) is satisfied by looking at Him and (3) the Srestha (the foremost) whom only physical contact may satisfy, sneha itself is of two types; one, ghrta-sneha, like ghrta, a basic material is respectful (adara-mava), but having no intrinsic sensation cannot cause excitement, the second type called madhu-sneha, whose all prevading capacity of excitement is self-evident as the sweetness of honey (madhu).

(3) Mana is the highest manifestation of sneha which can contain in itself even the lack of generous response to Krsna. It may be the development of either ghrta-sneha or madhu-sneha, and is known as udatta or lalita respectively.

(4) Pranava is the product of mana. when the latter gives way to unreserved confidence in Krsna’s love, and is of two kinds, namely, maitra (friendship) qualified by humility) and sakhya (unalloyed comaradship). Sakhya and maitra may become sumaitra and su-sakhya, that is excellent, when acted upon by udatta and lalita-mana.

(5) Raga is the final development of pranava when to be happy is to be aware of the acute pangs of love. The stages of raga are represented by visual colour, symbols, namely, nilima (blue), and raktima (blood-red). Nilima again may be deep blue (nili) or of a softer shade (syama) difficult to produce. The raktima may be like saffron (kusumbha) which is permanent, but spreads quickly and reflects other ragas or it may be like the colour produced by madder (manjistha) of all pervasive, independent and of increasing glamour. The nila-raga is representative of Krsna and Candravali while the manjistha raga represents Radha-Rrsna.

(6) Anuraga is the stage of love when it attracts to itself a sensation of unceasing novelty, a constant feeling of something not known before. Its manifestations are mutual self-surrender (paraspara-vasi-bhava) forebading of separation (prema-vaicittya) desire for being born as an inanimate object dear to Krsna (aprani-Janma) and day-dreaming of Krsna during sepration (vipralambha-visphurati).

(7) Bhava is anuraga sui juris, a substantive state svasvamvedya-dasa, when it ceases to need a substratum for itself, it is known a (8) mahabhava. It is very difficult even for Krsna’s queens to attain the stage of bhava which can be reached only by the milk-maids of Vrndavana. Maha-bhava is of two types (i) rudha and (ii) adhirudah.

(i) When the sattvikabhavas are as consuming as a raging fire, the mahabhava is called rudha. Its characteristics are (a) extreme piquancy, when separation even for a moment becomes unbearable, (b) capacity to stir the hearts of all present, (c) to make a whole age appear as a moment, and a moment appear as an age, (d) extreme depression even in a state of happiness due to a presentiment of malaise, (e) to be oblivious of self and of every thing even though not unconscious (mohadya bhavepi).

(ii) adhirudha is that stage when the passion is no longer raging like a fire, indeed its characteristic is that, it is indescribable, it is of two types, viz. (a) modana and (ii) madana. Modana involves the special charm of uddipta-sattvika and is felt by only those who belong to Radha’s yutha (group). In separation, it deepens into

Mohana which causes a yet greater intensity of uddinata-sattivika. It is characterised by Krsna’s fainting (consequent to a sense of separation from Radha) even when united in embrace of his queens at Dvaraka, Radha’s desire for happiness even by undergoing unbearable suffering, spreading sorrow over the whole world, causing weeping of the animals, even, craving death for elemental union, and divine frenzy (divyonmada). This mohana is usually seen only in the queen of Vrndavan, viz. Sri Radha. The divine frenzy of divyonmada is manifested in the forms of udghurana or citra-jalpa which means the performance of various irrational acts, such as getting ready to receive Krsna, when He is absent, being at Mathura. Citra-jalpa has ten forms viz. prajalpa, parijalpa, vijalpa, ujjalpa, samjalpa, avijalpa, adhijalpa, ajalapa, prati-jalap, sujalapa.

But the climax of the bhavas is reached in madana and it shines only in Radha. The characteristics of this state are irrational jealousy and remniscent contemplation of everything related to Krsna. It is indeed, a form of Yoga (yoga eva bhavedesa) therefore; there can be no separation in this stage of Bhava. And in its infinite forms it is eternal (nitya-lila).

This sthayi-bhava of madhura rati becomes madhura or srangara-rasa the highest type of erotic love. It is two fold; according as it is, love-in-union (sambhoga) and love-in-separation (vipralambha). The importance of vipralambha is that without separtion, union looses its charm. Separation indeed, is like the basic layer of colour, on which the tints are laid to bring out a better hue.

vipralambha has four forms, (i) purvaraga, (ii) mana, (iii) prema-vaicittya and (iv) pravasa, purva-raga is the stage of counting, where incipient love take root through seeing, hearing etc. “Seeing” here includes actual meeting, or seeing Krsna’s picture, or His vision in a dream. Hearing of Krsna through intermediaries.

All the factors which bring about the growth of madhura-rati, like abhiyoga ets, as mentioned above, are also present here. At the outset of love, the beautiful maiden is in full bloom of health. But with the progress of deepening emotion she begins to lose her weight, her peace of mind, as also sleep. Then she falls into a miserable stupor, verges towards madness, and loses consciouness and ultimately gives up life. These stages are differently manifested in different types of heroines, namely, praudha, samanjasa and sadharani.

The praudhapurva-raga begins with desire and ends with death, samanjasa’s manifestations are slightly different but here, too, the raga begins with desire and ends with death. The sadharani rati begins with desire, and her developing passion reaches its most critical stage in lamentation.

Mana is an essential ingredient of love-making, and marks the stage when the course of progressive intimacy is jolted by a misunderstanding, which may be baseless or may have some apparent basis. If the misunderstanding is baseless it is easily removed, but if there is some basis, it may be removed by sweet words, presents, praising oneself etc.

Prema-vaicittya is the ever present mutual fear of losing the dear one, even when the couple is together.

Pravasa is a deteriorative psychosis brought about by separation irrespective of the distance and time, the separation may be deliberate or forced . The ten stages of this condition are, brooding, sleeplessness, anxiety, and loss of weight, paleness, lamentations, illness, insanity unconsciousness and death.

But this separation (viraha) only happens in Krsna’s prakata (manifest) Ida, which is held simultaneously at Vraja, Dvaraka, and Vrindavana, the aprakata (unmanifest) lila is eternal, and in it, there are no pangs of separation.

(i) sambhoga is love making, and can be mukhya (real) or gauna (apparent as in a dream). Real lovemaking may be of short-duration (samksipta), or with mixed feeling as in a union after separation, (samkirana), or flourshing (sampanna) as after return from a short journey, or the union may be so completely satisfactory as to verge on satiety, which happens after a long absence abroad, samrddhimat.

Besides these primary rasas, there are seven gauna or subsidiary rasas according to the Gaudiyas. They differ from the primary or mukhya rasas in an important respect. So far as the mukhya or primary rasas are concerned, each of them is a permanent trait of some particular devotee, according to his inherent nature or temperament. Thus, some are temperamentally disposed the dasya-bhava, while some others again to devotion to Lord as a companion sakha and so on. They are thus, more or less permanent traits that characterise the activities of the respective devotees and accordingly the devotees may be classified in accordance with the kind of devotion to which they are temperamentally suited. But as regards the gauna-bhakti-rasas, they are neither permanent traits nor the devotees may be designated as hasya-bhakta or vira-bhakta, etc. after gauna-bhakti-rasa.

In other words, the gauna-bhakti-rasa are occasional in their appearance though as partaking of the camatkaritva or wonder causing element of aesthetic enjoyment, they deserve to rank as rasas or aesthetic enjoyment. The seven gauna-rasas are: (i) hasya or comic or laughter-provoking, (ii) adbhuta or possessing numinous quality without parallel in our work-a-day experience, (iii) vira-or the delight in valour and courage,

(iv) bhayanaka or the sentiment of terror-inspiring,

(v) bibhatsa or the sentiment of loathing, (vi) Raudra or the sentiment of wrath, anger, etc., and lastly (vii) karuna or the sentiment of pathos and compassion. As these rasas are well known and have been dealt with in the works of Sanskrit poetics, we need not enter into their detailed examination.

## Conclusion

In the foregoing pages we have examined the conception of Mukti in detail, as described in the Sanskrit texts of Gaudiya Vaisnavism in the background of other Vaisnava Systems as well as Orthodox System of Indian Philosophy. We have also shown how bhakti in Gaudiya Vaisnavism has been considered not only as means for the realisation of supreme end but also as an end-in-itself. It has also been made clear that all Vaisnava systems in general, Gaudiya Vaisnavism in particular consider mukti to be a positive state of existence in which the sadhaka partakes in the divine bliss consequent on his coming in close contact with the Supreme Person viz. Lord Krsna. The Gaudiya Acaryas have given a graphic description of the supreme realisation by an individual sadhaka when he transcends the mundane sphere, enters as it were, the divine realm, the abode of God, viz. Goloka.

The journey of a sadhaka has been described allegorically in the Brhad-bhagavata-mrtam through the story of a Gopa-Kumar who makes a trip to Goloka in search of Lord Krsna. It has been said that the sadhaka after receiving divine grace of the Lord has to traverse the different lokas that are above the mundane sphere viz. Bhuh Bhuvah, Svarga, Mahar, Jana, Tapas and Satva. All of the seven lokas are enveloped by a sheath or avarana of prakrti all the seven being enveloped by another sheath making a total of eight avarnas of Prakrti. The sadhaka has to pierce through these avaranas beyond them there is an ocean called Karanarnava or Viraia which has to be crossed before he can enter the pure order of nirvisesa Brahmam technically called Siddha Loka (Brahma-Loka). This loka is the goal of such sadhakas who aspire for knowledge (jnana) or those who wish to obtain sayujya mukti. The sadhakas attaining this loka realize cit-svarupa of Brahma minus all the display of Sakti. This state implies a kind of mergence of the individual sadhakas in Brahman, the absolute. It is believed that the asuras killed by Lord Krsna Himself also attain this loka, besides those following the path of knowledge. Beyond this lies the loka of Siva.

Vaikuntha which is the abode of Narayana and Laksami lies beyond Siva-loka. The sadhaka on reaching there finds Narayana and Laksami attended by three Saktis namely, Sri, Bhu, and Lila, sadhakas who follow vaidhi-Bhakti attain this loka where they visualise cit-sakti vilasa or display of the intrinsic power of the Lord. This is in the form of divine glory (aisvarya) of the Lord. It is here that the some of the avataras of Bhagavan reside with their respective retinues (parsadas). This is also known as parama-woman. The sadhaka tastes the bliss arising out of four kinds of mukti viz. salokva, samipya, sarupya and sarsti in this loka.

The highest loka however, is Krsna-loka or Goloka, the abode of Lord Himself.

This loka is characterised by Lord’s Hladini-sakti, the intrinsic power of the Divine Bliss. It is said that the sadhakas can get a glimpse of this loka but they cannot enter it; as they approach near this loka, they visualise the madhurya of the Lord and experience the highest bliss.

It is believed by the Gaudiyas that there exist in the ascending order, four Lokas viz. Ayodhya, Dvaraka, and Vrndavana in the heavenly abode of Lord Krsna whose replica is found on the terrestrial plane in the four holy bhumis of the Lord. It is said that a sadhaka following the vaidhi bhakti can go up to Ayodhya only where he can visualise the divine glory of the Lord while the sadhakas following the vaidhi raga-bhakti can go beyond Avodhva and enter into what has been called as the antah-pura of the Lord. If their bhakti is suhrd-rasa-nistha they can visualise Lord’s Lila in Dvaraka and Mathura, but if it is prema-rasa-nistha then, they can approach the highest-loka, technically called nitya-Vrndavana. The entry to higher abodes viz. Dvaraka and Mathura is restricted to sadhakas possesing either Dasya, Vatsala, Sakhya or Madhura-bhavas only.

Thus, the Gaudiyas admit two kinds of sadhakas -the higher type of sadhaka or bhakta who possessing either of the three bhavas, Vatsala, Sakhya or Madhura, follow the vaidhi-raga-bhakti or suddha-raga-bhakti, experience the display of divine madhurya of the Lord and enjoy the divine bliss arising out of the operation of Hladini Sakti; the lower kind of bhaktas possessing such nistha as seva (service) of the Lord and following vaidhi-bhakti have Ayodhya as culminating point of ascent where they visualise the glory of the Lord and enjoy the bliss arising out of either the four kinds of mukti (Salokya, Samipya, Sarupya and Sarsti).

As has been already indicated the four lokas viz. Ayodhya, Dvaraka, Mathura and Vrndavana, exist on two planes (i) celestial and terrestrial. The difference between the Lord’s Abode on celestial and terrestrial planes lies in the fact that in the former, Lord’s aprakrta-lila is being displayed eternally, while on the latter, prakrta lila is manifested, aprakrta-lila is also manifested occasionally. The Gaudiyas believe that the Sadhakas, on approaching the celestial plane can witness the aprakarta-lila that goes on eternally, but they cannot partake in the Lila.

The participants in the Lord’s Lila-vilasa are the innumerable Gopis, eight principal Sakhis. Sri Radha and none-else. The sadhakas who follow madhura-bhakti can serve the gopis, sakhis and even Sri Radha by identifying themselves with their bhavas (tad-tad-bhavanvitas), but they cannot actively join or participate in the eternal sport of the Lord.This is because the Gaudiyas make a distinction between two types of resident in Goloka-or Nitya Vrndavana.

The Gopas and Gopis etc. belong to the higher category, technically called ‘Isvara Koti’ the rest fall under the category known as ‘jiva-Koti’. Those who come under the former category are select-few who in fact, represent a mode of the operation of svarupa-sakti of the Lord e.g., the parikaras or retinues of the Lord, who are eternal and resemble Him in all respects such as in prakrti vesa and lila. But, those belonging the cateogy of jiva-Koti are previleged few who reach the highest state through the successive development of Sadhana-bhakti of Raganuga-tvpe. Such jivas attain the position of eternal attendants of the Lord.

The Sadhakas have freedom to adopt any of the five bhavas viz. Santa, Prita, Prevas, Vatsala and Madhura, the position attained by them is the same. But those who follow madhura-bhava attain the position of manjari - a kind of eternal maid-servants of Sri Radha - the eternal consort of the Lord and is the Sakti Herself in its fullest amplitutde. The term for eternal servants of the Lord Implies only to those who worship the Lord through Prita or Dasya-bhava.

It may be noted that the entire nitya Vrndavan-Lila has been represented symbolically on thousands petal-lotus, at the centre of which ‘Sri Krsna and Sri Radha’, take their position, and the eight principal Sakhis viz. Lalita, Visakha, Citra, Campaka-vallika, Tunga-vidya, Indu-lekha, Ranga-devi and Sudevi, with their retinues stand around them (Sri Radha & Sri Krsna). These eight principal Sakhis (asta-sakhis) are nitya-siddhas who enjoy the Divine Bliss by merely identifying themselves with one of the bhavas of Sri Radha, and thus individually partaking in the lila-vilasa. These asta-sakhis however, are always assisted by two sets of manjaris who take their position between the Lord and Sri Radha, and the Sakhis as well on the outer periphery of the lotus.

The principal-eight-manjaris take their position near the Kinjalka - i.e., between the eight principal sakhis and Vrndavanesvari-Sri Radha. They are known as Lavanga-manjari, Rupa manjari, Guna-manjari, Rati-mahjari Bhadra-manari, Lila-manjari and Vilasa-manjari.

These manjaris who occupy the position of outer peripheries of the Lotus are called kinkaries. Both these categories of manjaries personify the service of Sri Yugala, i.e., Radha and Krsna.

Since the Gaudiyas formulate the theory of nitya-dastva, they consider the attainment of manjirtva to be the supreme ideal, the culmination of their sadhana. This is in consonance with their doctrine of anyabhi-lasitasunyatvam.

In fact the use of singular number in the term of purusa is significant. Although the Karika 18 gives a number of hetus to prove the plurality of purusa but these arguments apply only to embodied purusa and not to purusa as such. Some commentators like Mathara & Gaurapada infact seem to believe in the existence of one purusa. See STK (Jha’s English translation) notes on p.17.

Vedanta Sara p.2.

cidacidrupo’ pi pratisariram bhinnah vibhusca.....Advaitabrahma-siddhi, quoted in EH. p. 230.

tatrapi cidamsena drastrtvam soham iti pratyabhijna visayatvam ca. acidamsena jnanasukhadi-rupen aparinamitvam. sah (atma) ahampratyayenaiva vedyah. Ibid. p. 230.

atyantika karmocchedarupamuktau indriyadirahitasya jnanasambhavat jadatvena tadr satmapraptih apurusarthatvat pravrttireva sreyasi na nivrttih- Quoted in EH, p. 231.

tasmanna prapancavilayo moksah kintu prapancasambandha-vijayah—op.cit J,1,5,5, p. 358.

avidya-nirmito hi prapancah, svapnaprapancavat, prabodhena brahmavidyayavidyayam vilinayam svayameva viliyate. Ibid. p. 358.

jnanasantanasya nissesa vasanocchedat nilapitadivaicitryam hitva kevalam savinmatrena avasthanam iti kecit, Ibid. p. 356.

apare tu dipasantanasyaivajnana-santanesya uparamah Ibid.356

tredha hi prapancah purusam badhnati-bhogayatanam sariram, bhoga-sadhanani indriyani, bhogyah sabdadayah visayah, bhoga iti ca sukhaduhkhavisayo’ paroksanubhava ucyate, Ibid. p. 358.

tadasya trividhasya bandhasya atyantiko vilayah moksah, Ibid, p. 358.

purvotapannanam sarirendriyavisayanam vinasah anutpannanam ca atyantiko'nutpadah. Ibid.356.

utpadakayordharmadharmayoh nihsesayoh pariksayat, soyam prapancasambandho bandhah tadvimoksah ca moksah. Ibid. p. 358.

nanu evamasesadharma dharmaksayat muktasya na kimcit. sukham syat, tatasca apurusartho moksah syat? Ibid. p. 358.

na esa dosah-nahi dharmajanyo muktasyanandah tatha satyutpattimatvadvinasi syat tatra apunavrtti sruter virodhah syat..............svabhavikastu atmanandah samsarendbhibhutah san nirmukta-samsarasyabhivyakto bhogyo bhavati svabhavikatve canandasya ‘anandam Brahma” iti. Ibid. p. 358.

nanu sukhabhavepi srutirasti - “asariram vavasantam na priya-priye sprsatah” iti ? satyam- visayasukhabhiprayam tvevam jatiyakam, anyatha anandasrutivirodhat – Ibid. p. 359.

atha sukhabhava vacana balenananda vacanameva duhkha-bhavabhiprayam kimiti na vyakhyayate ? sukhabhava vacananamatyalpatvat, tesamevanyatha karanamyuktamna, bhuyasamananda vacananam, kinca ananda sabdasya duhkhabhava paratve-atyanta svarthahanih syat, sukha sabdasya tu samanyavacansya visesavyakhyane' pi nativa svarthahaniriti tadeva yuktam. Ibid. p. 359.

nanu vidyamanopyanando’ anubhuyamano’ kincitkara eva, na ca muktasya sarvakarana hinasya anandanubhavah sambhavati. sva prakaso’ yamananda iti cet ? na, sarsaravasthayamapraka-sat. nanu tatrapi anandasvabhavah purusah prakasate eva atmani premotpatteh,na ’ hi asukhe premotpattih sambhavati napi avijnate sukhe. Ibid. p. 359.

kimidanimasmabhiratma sukharupo’nubhuyate ? satyamiti-bruvana-syatisahasikasya nottaram vacyam. yadi samsarino'pi paramanandamanubhaveyuh tatomuktasya na kascidatisaya iti, tadeva apurusarthatvam moksasyapadyate, ato baladasukha-rupasyapi atmanah premavisayatvam drstatvadangi kartavyam. Ibid. p. 360.

atha, svaprakaso’ pyanandah samsaravasthayamabhibhuto na prakasate ko’ yam prakasamanasyabhibhavo nama ? Ibid. p. 360.

prakasanivaranam hi abhibhavah, na ca prakasamanasyaprakasanam sambhavati, yadi tu naiva prakasate samsaravasthayam tasyamavasthayamasatah prakasasya muktavasthayamutpadyamanasya karanam vaktavyam, vijnanameva ca prakasa-khyasya phalasya karanam taccendriy-adhinam, na ca muktasyendriyani sambhavanti kathamanandanubhavah syat ? Ibid. p. 360.

ucyate-bahyendriyanyeva muktasya nivartante, manastu tasyama-vasthayamanuvartate ity ananda srutibaladevadhyavasiyate. evam jnanam ca ‘nahi vijnaturvijnaterviparilopo vidyate’ iti sruteh, tasmanmuktavasthayam manasapratyaks ena paramanandamanubh-avannatmavatisthate. Ibid. p. 360 (16-19), p. 361 (1-2)

taduktam - “nijam yattvatmacaitanyamanandasca isyate ca yah. yacca nityavibhutvadi-.......... tairatma naivamucyate, iti............... Ibid. p. 361.

tasmatkarmaksayadeva hetvabhavenamucyate, na hi abhavatmakam muktva moksanityatva karanam” (Slokavartika). quoted in Ibid. p. 361.

apare tvahuh-abhavatmakatvavacanameva svamatamupapattyabhidhanat, anandavacanam tu panyasamatratvat paramatam, na hi muktasyanandanubhavah sambhavati karanabhavat. manah syat, iti ceta ? na, amanaskatva sruteh "amano’vak’" iti. ' Ibid, p.' 362 (3-7).

"nahi vijnaturvijnaterviparilopo vidyate’ iti, tajjnanasaktyabhiprayam, anyatha hi susuptavapi jnananuvrttirukta syat sa ca samvid-virudha ityuktam. vispastam casya sakty abhipra-patvam, evam hi sruyateyadvaitanna pasyati pasyan vaitanna-pasyati, na hi drasturdrster viparilopo vidyate, avinastvat, - — na srotur sruter viparilopo vidyate’ iti. etc. Ibid. p. 362.

tatra tatra drsti----sruti----- caksuhsrotra----- indriyajanya, nam-rupa — sabda — visaya-nam vijnanavisesanam vacakanamarthantara- visayanam,napi vijnanasamanyasya. Ibid. p. 363.

na caisam jnanavisesanam susptau muktau va katham cidapya parilopah sambhavati na hi tadanimindriyani santi, napi gandhadayo jnayante. Ibid. p. 363 (4-8).

yattvadityaprakasakr tvattvaditi, kim tasya prakasakatvamabhipretam yadi timirotsaranam? tannityamastyeva sarvada hi asavavyavahitesu desesu timiramutsadayatyeva, natvevamatma sarvada srnoti susuptavapi sravanat, atha jnanajanakatvamadityasya prakasakatvam? na tannityam, tatsamarthyameva tatrapinityam, tasmajjnanasaktyabhiprayamidam nityatvabh-idhanam. Ibid. p. 364. (3-9).

yadetat susuptau muktau vatmana pasyati pasyanneva - na pasyati nahi drasturatmano ya darsana saktistasyah kadacidapi lopo vidyate, sa hi avinasiniti; Ibid, 364 (10-13).

tatha jnanasaktyaparilopa eva jnanaparilopa ucyate, Ibid. p. 365 (1-2).

atratmatattvam sarvasvavasthasu vijnanaya samarthyamiti arthastena vijnanaghanatvabh-idhanam samarthyabhiprayamiti vyakhyatam bhavati, sambhavati ca fhktasyapi jnanabhava, iti nasti virodhah ----- etc. Ibid. p. 366.

yadi saktirasti tasyamavasthayam tatah kena jnananutpattih ? jnanasadhananamindriyanam jneyanam cavisayanamabhavat-------sadhanabhavannirakrte satiatmanameva muktavasthayam janatu etc.............na hiatmapi vinasadhanena jnatum sakyate, manasakhalvasausamsaravasthayam jnayate. na ca muktasyamanassambandhosti, atah................vyakta evamuktasyatmajnanabhavo jnanaiaktimatrasyavasthanamsrutya darsitam, tasmat nihsambandho niranandas’ camoksah Ibid. p. 366.

duhkhatyanta samucchede-sati-pragatma-vartinah anandasyanubhutis(tu) muktirukta kumarilaih”, Manameyodaya p. 88.

etenabudhyadivisesagunavilaye satyatmanah svarupa vasthanam moksah- Ibid. p. 88,

asya jivasyaisvaryadi tirohitam, tatra hetuh parabhidhaynat, parasya bhagavato’ bhitodhyanam svasya jivasya ca sarvato bhogeccha tasmaddhetoh.- Ibid. p.81.

aisvaryadi sadbhave vaicitryabhavad bhogo na bhavisyatiti jive tirobhavam kritavan svadharmanam. tata eva tirobhavad bandho bhagavaddharma viparitadharmavattvam cetyarthah. Ibid, p.81

tatra svarupatmakena sadansena jadodgamah purvamuktah.................. ato jadesu sanmatra karyatvaccittirobhavah Ibid. p.86.

tathahi karana bhutadaksarabhrahmanah sakasadyathagneheksudra visphulingah vyuccarantiti amteh saccidanandatmako’ anranso nihsarati. vyuccarana’ nantaram karanarupa ksaragatasya, visuddhasattvasyan sabhutena tadrkasattvena bhagavadicchaya prabalikrtenan-andareastirobhavati. tada irupadhiko’ anurupo' ksaransascit pradhanastirohitanando jivasabda vacyo bhavati”. op. cit. p.7.

taduktam vedastuti subodhinyam, svakrta puresvamisvityatra, jivo nam bhagavatascidarea iti. Ibid. p. 7.

ato vyuccarane satyanandan s'a tirodhanajjivatvam. vyuccaranottaram, anandahsatirodhane satyavidya sarhbandhat, purva varttinyamavasthayam suddha jiva. iti vyavaharah .... jivasuddhatvam tvavidya sambandharahityam. tato’sminjivarupe bhagavadanse bhagavadaisvaryadisadagunamhsabhutanamaisvaryadinam haricchaya tirobhavah. parabhidhyanattu tirohitam, tato hyasyabandhaviparyaya Ibid. p.7.

tada tesam madhye kesucijji vesu ramanecchaya vicaritasya bahutvabhavanasya siddhaye uccabhaveccha visayibhutam muktyadhikararupam suksma sadvasana visistam daivatvam sampadayati bhagavan. tadaiva jiva muktiyogya bhavanti. Ibid, p.7-8.

tato hyavidyasambandhad bandhah bandho’syavidyayanadiriti - - - anaditvam tu karyantarapeksaya. Ibid. p.8.

tato dehendriyantah karanadhyasah svaru pavismrtiscetyavidyaya pancaparvani. tair baddho duhkhitah. asya jivasamsara ucyata iti nibandhat. ucyate, natujayate. abhimatyatmakatvat. Ibid. p.8.

tatah suksmasthuladehasambandhat samsaridharmajjanmamaranadinanubhavan bhagavat krpaya satsangadi labdhva pancaparvatmika vidyam prapya paramananda-laksanam muktim labhate. “vairagyam sahkhayayogau ca tapo bhaktisca keiave”. pancaparveti vidyeyam yaya vidvan harimvisaditivakyat. tatra yavat pancaparvatmikam vidyam prapnuyat tavat samsariti vyavaharah. tadagre mukta iti sadtriyo vyavaharah Ibid. p.8.

sa ca muktajivo dvividhah. Jivanmukta muktasca. Tatra sanakadayo gatavidyaste jivanmukta ucyante. ye tu vyapivaikunthetara bhagavallokavasinaste mukta ityucyante. tatah parama krpaya paramamuktistatra tu suddhabrahmatvameveti nirnayah. keciduttama devastu satsanga di prapya margaucijanya sravanadi samudbhuta svatantra bhaktya phalarupaya nityalilayam pravisanti. sa tesam moksah. Ibid. p.8.

ye tu daivajivebhyo vyatirikastesam sadgunatirodhananantaram, avidyasambandhe nica-bhaveccha visayibhutam muktipratibandhakamasadvasana visistamasuratvam sampadayati bhagavan tada te asurajiva ucyante. te hyasadvasanavasattadr asam sthuladeharh prapya ninditakarmaniratah santo nicayonigahbhavanti. te sarvada samsarina eva. ---- tesam tu tadaivavidyakaryanam yada bhagavanatmaraticchurbhavati tada bhagavanavidyakaryam samsaram sarvatra sthitam svayameva jivakrtasadhananapekso nasayati. tada tes masuranam sarvadosanam nasat punah suddha jivatvam bhavati. Ibid. p.8.

idamatrajneyam. Jiva dvividhah, daivah asurah. daivapi dvividhah. maryadamargiyah pustimargisyas’ca sa ca bhagavatavidyasambandhat purvam daivatvasampadana nantaram maryada margiya jivebhyo bhettum visesanugrahecchaya pustimargiyaphalam prapayisyatyevam. maryadamargiyajivastu jnanabhakti-karma-yogadibhih sacchastra pratipaditair yathayatham milita bahuvidha jneyah. te' pi muktavadhikarinah. ato mukti pusti-margiyanam maryada margiyanam ca.
